{"command":"& \"C:\\ado\\win-dev-skills-benchmark\\agent-benchmark\\results\\run12\\000_ApplicationResources\\app\\.github\\skills\\winui-uwp-migration\\scripts\\Get-MigrationPattern.ps1\" -Anchor \"getforcurrentview\"","description":"Fetch getforcurrentview migration pattern","mode":"sync"}
--- RESULT ---
<a id="getforcurrentview"></a>
## GetForCurrentView() ReplacementsNone of the `GetForCurrentView()` patterns work in WinUI 3 desktop — there is no implicit per-view singleton.| UWP API | WinUI 3 Replacement |
|---------|---------------------|
| `ApplicationView.GetForCurrentView()` | `AppWindow.GetFromWindowId(windowId)` |
| `UIViewSettings.GetForCurrentView()` | `AppWindow` properties (size, presenter) |
| `DisplayInformation.GetForCurrentView()` | `XamlRoot.RasterizationScale` or Win32 `GetDpiForWindow` |
| `CoreApplication.GetCurrentView()` | Track windows manually in `App` |
| `SystemNavigationManager.GetForCurrentView()` | Wire back handling in `NavigationView` / `BackRequested` directly |
<exited with exit code 0>

=== TURN 8 ===

--- TOOL: powershell ---
{"command":"& \"C:\\ado\\win-dev-skills-benchmark\\agent-benchmark\\results\\run12\\000_ApplicationResources\\app\\.github\\skills\\winui-uwp-migration\\scripts\\Get-MigrationPattern.ps1\" -Anchor \"threading\"","description":"Fetch threading migration pattern","mode":"sync"}
--- RESULT ---
<a id="threading"></a>
## Threading: CoreDispatcher → DispatcherQueueUWP (replace this):```csharp
await Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => StatusText.Text = "Done");
```WinUI 3:```csharp
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(() => StatusText.Text = "Done");
DispatcherQueue.TryEnqueue(DispatcherQueuePriority.High, () => ProgressBar.Value = 100);
```Cache the queue off the UI thread via `DispatcherQueue.GetForCurrentThread()`. UWP's ASTA reentrancy protection is gone — watch for reentrancy in async code that pumps messages. See the official [threading guide](https://learn.microsoft.com/windows/apps/windows-app-sdk/migrate-to-windows-app-sdk/guides/threading).
<exited with exit code 0>

10.     public static class LocalizedNamesLibrary
11.     {
12.         static ResourceLoader  resourceLoader = null;
13. 
14.         public static string LibraryName { 
15.             get 
16.             {
17.                 String name;
18.                 GetLibraryName("string1", out name);
19.                 return name;
20.             } 
21.         }
22. 
23.         private static void GetLibraryName(string resourceName, out string resourceValue)
24.         {
25.             if (resourceLoader == null)
26.             {
27.                 // TODO[migrate-001]: see PATTERNS.md#getforcurrentview
28.                 resourceLoader = ResourceLoader.GetForCurrentView("AppResourceClassLibrary/Resources");
29.             }
30.             resourceValue = resourceLoader.GetString(resourceName);
31.         }

76.                 {
77.                     context.QualifierValues["contrast"] = selectedContrast.ToString();
78.                 }
79.                 if (selectedHomeRegion != null)
80.                 {
81.                     context.QualifierValues["homeregion"] = selectedHomeRegion.ToString();
82.                 }
83. 
84.                 Scenario10_SearchMultipleResourceIds(context, new string[] { "LanguageOnly", "ScaleOnly", "ContrastOnly", "HomeRegionOnly", "MultiDimensional" });
85.             }
86.         }
87. 
88.         void Scenario10_SearchMultipleResourceIds(ResourceContext context, string[] resourceIds)
89.         {
90.             this.Scenario10TextBlock.Text = "";
91.             var dimensionMap = ResourceManager.Current.MainResourceMap.GetSubtree("dimensions");
92. 
93.             foreach (var id in resourceIds)
94.             {
95.                 NamedResource namedResource;
96.                 if (dimensionMap.TryGetValue(id, out namedResource))
97.                 {
98.                     var resourceCandidates = namedResource.ResolveAll(context);
99.                     Scenario10_ShowCandidates(id, resourceCandidates);
100.                 }
101.             }
102.         }
103. 
104.         void Scenario10_ShowCandidates(string resourceId, IReadOnlyList<ResourceCandidate> resourceCandidates)
105.         {
106.             // print 'resourceId', 'found value', 'qualifer info', 'matching condition'
107.             string outText = "resourceId: dimensions\\" + resourceId + "\r\n";
108.             int i = 0;
109.             foreach (var candidate in resourceCandidates)
110.             {
111.                 var value = candidate.ValueAsString;
112.                 outText += "    Candidate " + i.ToString() + ":" + value + "\r\n";
113.                 int j = 0;
114.                 foreach (var qualifier in candidate.Qualifiers)
115.                 {
116.                     var qualifierName = qualifier.QualifierName;
117.                     var qualifierValue = qualifier.QualifierValue;
118.                     outText += "        Qualifer: " + qualifierName + ": " + qualifierValue + "\r\n";
119.                     outText += "        Matching: IsMatch (" + qualifier.IsMatch.ToString() + ")  " + "IsDefault (" + qualifier.IsDefault.ToString() + ")" + "\r\n";
120.                     j++;
121.                 }
122.                 i++;
123.             }
124. 
125.             this.Scenario10TextBlock.Text += outText + "\r\n";
126. 
127.         }
128. 
129.     }
130. }
131.

22.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
23.     /// </summary>
24.     public sealed partial class Scenario12 : Page
25.     {
26.         // A pointer back to the main page.  This is needed if you want to call methods in MainPage such
27.         // as NotifyUser()
28.         MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
29. 
30.         public Scenario12()
31.         {
32.             this.InitializeComponent();
33.         }
34. 
35.         /// <summary>
36.         /// Invoked when this page is about to be displayed in a Frame.
37.         /// </summary>
38.         /// <param name="e">Event data that describes how this page was reached.  The Parameter
39.         /// property is typically used to configure the page.</param>
40.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
41.         {
42.         }
43. 
44.         private async void Scenario12Button_Show_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
45.         {
46.             // Two coding patterns will be used:
47.             //   1. Get a ResourceContext on the UI thread using GetForCurrentView and pass 
48.             //      to the non-UI thread
49.             //   2. Get a ResourceContext on the non-UI thread using GetForViewIndependentUse
50.             //
51.             // Two analogous patterns could be used for ResourceLoader instead of ResourceContext.
52. 
53. 
54.             // pattern 1: get a ResourceContext for the UI thread
55.             // TODO[migrate-004]: see PATTERNS.md#getforcurrentview
56.             ResourceContext defaultContextForUiThread = ResourceContext.GetForCurrentView();
57. 
58.             // pattern 2: we'll create a view-independent context in the non-UI worker thread
59. 
60. 
61.             // We need some things in order to display results in the UI (doing that
62.             // for purposes of this sample, to show that work was actually done in the
63.             // worker thread):
64.             List<string> uiDependentResourceList = new List<string>();
65.             List<string> uiIndependentResourceList = new List<string>();
66. 
67. 
68.             // use a worker thread for the heavy lifting so the UI isn't blocked
69.             await Windows.System.Threading.ThreadPool.RunAsync(
70.                 (source) =>
71.                 {
72.                     ResourceMap stringResourceMap = ResourceManager.Current.MainResourceMap.GetSubtree("Resources");
73. 
74.                     // pattern 1: the defaultContextForUiThread variable was created above and is visible here
75. 
76.                     // pattern 2: get a view-independent ResourceContext
77.                     ResourceContext defaultViewIndependentResourceContext = ResourceContext.GetForViewIndependentUse();
78. 
79.                     // NOTE: The ResourceContext obtained using GetForViewIndependentUse() has no scale qualifier
80.                     // value set. If this ResourceContext is used in its default state to retrieve a resource, that 
81.                     // will work provided that the resource does not have any scale-qualified variants. But if the
82.                     // resource has any scale-qualified variants, then that will fail at runtime.
83.                     //
84.                     // A scale qualifier value on this ResourceContext can be set programmatically. If that is done,
85.                     // then the ResourceContext can be used to retrieve a resource that has scale-qualified variants.
86.                     // But if the scale qualifier is reset (e.g., using the ResourceContext.Reset() method), then
87.                     // it will return to the default state with no scale qualifier value set, and cannot be used
88.                     // to retrieve any resource that has scale-qualified variants.
89. 
90. 
91.                     // simulate processing a number of items
92.                     // just using a single string resource: that's sufficient to demonstrate 
93.                     for (var i = 0; i < 4; i++)
94.                     {
95.                         // pattern 1: use the ResourceContext from the UI thread
96.                         string listItem1 = stringResourceMap.GetValue("string1", defaultContextForUiThread).ValueAsString;
97.                         uiDependentResourceList.Add(listItem1);
98. 
99.                         // pattern 2: use the view-independent ResourceContext
100.                         string listItem2 = stringResourceMap.GetValue("string1", defaultViewIndependentResourceContext).ValueAsString;
101.                         uiIndependentResourceList.Add(listItem2);
102.                     }
103. 
104.                 });
105. 
106.             // Display the results in one go. (A more finessed design might add results
107.             // in the UI asynchronously, but that goes beyond what this sample is 
108.             // demonstrating.)
109.             ViewDependentResourcesList.ItemsSource = uiDependentResourceList;
110.             ViewIndependentResourcesList.ItemsSource = uiIndependentResourceList;
111.         }
112. 
113.     }
114. }

27.         int lastSelectedIndex;
28. 
29.         public Scenario8()
30.         {
31.             this.InitializeComponent();
32.             PopulateComboBox();
33.             UpdateCurrentAppLanguageMessage();
34.         }
35. 
36.         private void ShowText()
37.         {
38.             // TODO[migrate-010]: see PATTERNS.md#getforcurrentview
39.             ResourceContext defaultContextForCurrentView = ResourceContext.GetForCurrentView();
40.             ResourceMap stringResourcesResourceMap = ResourceManager.Current.MainResourceMap.GetSubtree("Resources");
41.             Scenario8MessageTextBlock.Text = stringResourcesResourceMap.GetValue("string1", defaultContextForCurrentView).ValueAsString;
42.         }
43. 
44.         private void LanguageOverrideCombo_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
45.         {
46.             var combo = sender as ComboBox;
47.             var item = combo.SelectedValue as ComboBoxItem;
48.             var languageTag = item.Tag as string;
49. 
50.             // Ignore the divider (tag = "-")
51.             if (languageTag == "-")
52.             {
53.                 combo.SelectedIndex = lastSelectedIndex;
54.             }
55.             else
56.             {
57.                 lastSelectedIndex = combo.SelectedIndex;
58. 
59.                 // Set the persistent language override
60.                 ApplicationLanguages.PrimaryLanguageOverride = languageTag;
61. 
62.                 // update current app languages message
63.                 UpdateCurrentAppLanguageMessage();
64.             }
65.         }
66. 
67.         void AddItemForLanguageTag(string languageTag)
68.         {
69.             var language = new Language(languageTag);
70.             var item = new ComboBoxItem { Content = language.DisplayName, Tag = languageTag };
71.             LanguageOverrideCombo.Items.Add(item);
72. 
73.             // Select this item if it is the primary language override.
74.             if (languageTag == ApplicationLanguages.PrimaryLanguageOverride)
75.             {
76.                 LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectedItem = item;
77.             }
78.         }
79. 
80.         void PopulateComboBox()
81.         {
82.             // First show the default setting
83.             LanguageOverrideCombo.Items.Add(new ComboBoxItem { Content = "Use language preferences (recommended)", Tag = "" });
84.             LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectedIndex = 0;
85. 
86.             // If there are app languages that the user speaks, show them next
87. 
88.             // Note: the first (non-override) language, if set as the primary language override
89.             // would give the same result as not having any primary language override. There's
90.             // still a difference, though: If the user changes their language preferences, the 
91.             // default setting (no override) would mean that the actual primary app language
92.             // could change. But if it's set as an override, then it will remain the primary
93.             // app language after the user changes their language preferences.
94. 
95.             foreach (var languageTag in ApplicationLanguages.Languages)
96.             {
97.                 AddItemForLanguageTag(languageTag);
98.             }
99. 
100.             // Now add a divider followed by all the app manifest languages (in case the user
101.             // wants to pick a language not currently in their profile)
102. 
103.             // NOTE: If an app is deployed using a bundle with resource packages, the following
104.             // addition to the list may not be useful: The set of languages returned by 
105.             // ApplicationLanguages.ManifestLanguages will consist of only those manifest 
106.             // languages in the main app package or in the resource packages that are installed 
107.             // and registered for the current user. Language resource packages get deployed for 
108.             // the user if the language is in the user's profile. Therefore, the only difference 
109.             // from the set returned by ApplicationLanguages.Languages above would depend on 
110.             // which languages are included in the main app package.
111. 
112.             LanguageOverrideCombo.Items.Add(new ComboBoxItem { Content = "——————", Tag = "-" });
113. 
114.             // In a production app, this list should be sorted, but that's beyond the
115.             // focus of this sample.
116.             foreach (var languageTag in ApplicationLanguages.ManifestLanguages)
117.             {
118.                 AddItemForLanguageTag(languageTag);
119.             }
120. 
121.             LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectionChanged += LanguageOverrideCombo_SelectionChanged;
122. 
123.             lastSelectedIndex = LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectedIndex;
124.         }
125. 
126.         private void UpdateCurrentAppLanguageMessage()
127.         {
128.             this.Scenario8AppLanguagesTextBlock.Text = "Current app language(s): " + GetAppLanguagesAsFormattedString();
129.         }
130. 
131.         private string GetAppLanguagesAsFormattedString()
132.         {
133.             return String.Join(", ", ApplicationLanguages.Languages);
134.         }
135.     }

12. using System;
13. using System.Collections.Generic;
14. using Windows.UI.Core;
15. using Windows.UI.Xaml;
16. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Automation.Peers;
17. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Controls;
18. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Data;
19. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Media;
20. using Windows.UI.Xaml.Navigation;
21. 
22. // The Blank Page item template is documented at http://go.microsoft.com/fwlink/?LinkId=402352&clcid=0x409
23. 
24. namespace SDKTemplate
25. {
26.     /// <summary>
27.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
28.     /// </summary>
29.     public sealed partial class MainPage : Page
30.     {
31.         public static MainPage Current;
32. 
33.         public MainPage()
34.         {
35.             this.InitializeComponent();
36. 
37.             // This is a static public property that allows downstream pages to get a handle to the MainPage instance
38.             // in order to call methods that are in this class.
39.             Current = this;
40.             SampleTitle.Text = FEATURE_NAME;
41.         }
42. 
43.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
44.         {
45.             // Populate the scenario list from the SampleConfiguration.cs file
46.             var itemCollection = new List<Scenario>();
47.             int i = 1;
48.             foreach (Scenario s in scenarios)
49.             {
50.                 itemCollection.Add(new Scenario { Title = $"{i++}) {s.Title}", ClassType = s.ClassType });
51.             }
52.             ScenarioControl.ItemsSource = itemCollection;
53. 
54.             if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
55.             {
56.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = -1;
57.             }
58.             else
59.             {
60.                 ScenarioControl.SelectedIndex = 0;
61.             }
62.         }
63. 
64.         /// <summary>
65.         /// Called whenever the user changes selection in the scenarios list.  This method will navigate to the respective
66.         /// sample scenario page.
67.         /// </summary>
68.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
69.         /// <param name="e"></param>
70.         private void ScenarioControl_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
71.         {
72.             // Clear the status block when navigating scenarios.
73.             NotifyUser(String.Empty, NotifyType.StatusMessage);
74. 
75.             ListBox scenarioListBox = sender as ListBox;
76.             Scenario s = scenarioListBox.SelectedItem as Scenario;
77.             if (s != null)
78.             {
79.                 ScenarioFrame.Navigate(s.ClassType);
80.                 if (Window.Current.Bounds.Width < 640)
81.                 {
82.                     Splitter.IsPaneOpen = false;
83.                 }
84.             }
85.         }
86. 
87.         public List<Scenario> Scenarios
88.         {
89.             get { return this.scenarios; }
90.         }
91. 
92.         /// <summary>
93.         /// Display a message to the user.
94.         /// This method may be called from any thread.
95.         /// </summary>
96.         /// <param name="strMessage"></param>
97.         /// <param name="type"></param>
98.         public void NotifyUser(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
99.         {
100.             // If called from the UI thread, then update immediately.
101.             // Otherwise, schedule a task on the UI thread to perform the update.
102.             if (Dispatcher.HasThreadAccess)
103.             {
104.                 UpdateStatus(strMessage, type);
105.             }
106.             else
107.             {
108.                 var task = Dispatcher.RunAsync(CoreDispatcherPriority.Normal, () => UpdateStatus(strMessage, type));
109.             }
110.         }
111. 
112.         private void UpdateStatus(string strMessage, NotifyType type)
113.         {
114.             switch (type)
115.             {
116.                 case NotifyType.StatusMessage:
117.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Green);
118.                     break;
119.                 case NotifyType.ErrorMessage:
120.                     StatusBorder.Background = new SolidColorBrush(Windows.UI.Colors.Red);
121.                     break;
122.             }
123. 
124.             StatusBlock.Text = strMessage;
125. 
126.             // Collapse the StatusBlock if it has no text to conserve real estate.
127.             StatusBorder.Visibility = (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty) ? Visibility.Visible : Visibility.Collapsed;
128.             if (StatusBlock.Text != String.Empty)
129.             {
130.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
131.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Visible;
132.             }
133.             else
134.             {
135.                 StatusBorder.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
136.                 StatusPanel.Visibility = Visibility.Collapsed;
137.             }
138. 
139. 			// Raise an event if necessary to enable a screen reader to announce the status update.
140. 			var peer = FrameworkElementAutomationPeer.FromElement(StatusBlock);
141. 			if (peer != null)
142. 			{
143. 				peer.RaiseAutomationEvent(AutomationEvents.LiveRegionChanged);
144. 			}
145. 		}
146. 
147.         async void Footer_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
148.         {
149.             await Windows.System.Launcher.LaunchUriAsync(new Uri(((HyperlinkButton)sender).Tag.ToString()));
150.         }
151. 
152.         private void Button_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
153.         {
154.             Splitter.IsPaneOpen = !Splitter.IsPaneOpen;
155.         }
156.     }
157.     public enum NotifyType
158.     {
159.         StatusMessage,
160.         ErrorMessage
161.     };
162. }
163.

20. {
21.     /// <summary>
22.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
23.     /// </summary>
24.     public sealed partial class Scenario9 : Page
25.     {
26.         // A pointer back to the main page.  This is needed if you want to call methods in MainPage such
27.         // as NotifyUser()
28.         MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
29. 
30.         public Scenario9()
31.         {
32.             this.InitializeComponent();
33.         }
34. 
35.         /// <summary>
36.         /// Invoked when this page is about to be displayed in a Frame.
37.         /// </summary>
38.         /// <param name="e">Event data that describes how this page was reached.  The Parameter
39.         /// property is typically used to configure the page.</param>
40.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
41.         {
42.         }
43. 
44.         /// <summary>
45.         /// Invoked when the user has selected a different scenario and navigation away from
46.         /// this page is about to occur.
47.         /// </summary>
48.         /// <param name="e">Event data that describes the navigation that has unloaded this
49.         /// page.</param>
50.         protected override void OnNavigatedFrom(NavigationEventArgs e)
51.         {
52.             // Clearing qualifier value overrides set on the default context for the current
53.             // view so that an override set here doesn't impact other scenarios. See comments
54.             // below for additional information.
55.             ResourceContext.GetForCurrentView().Reset();
56.             base.OnNavigatedFrom(e);
57.         }
58. 
59.         /// <summary>
60.         /// This is the click handler for the 'Scenario9Button_Show' button.  You would replace this with your own handler
61.         /// if you have a button or buttons on this page.
62.         /// </summary>
63.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
64.         /// <param name="e"></param>
65.         private void Scenario9Button_Show_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
66.         {
67.             Button b = sender as Button;
68.             if (b != null)
69.             {
70.                 // A langauge override will be set on the default context for the current view.
71.                 // When navigating between different scenarios in this sample, the content for each
72.                 // scenario is loaded into a host page that remains constant. The view (meaning, the 
73.                 // CoreWindow) remains the same, and so it is the same default context that's in use. 
74.                 // Thus, an override set here can impact behavior in other scenarios.
75.                 //
76.                 // However, the description for the scenario may give the impression that a value 
77.                 // being set is local to this scenario. Also, when entering this scenario, the combo 
78.                 // box always gets set to the first item, which can add to the confusion. To avoid 
79.                 // confusion when using this sample, the override that gets set here will be cleared 
80.                 // when the user navigates away from this scenario (in the OnNavigatedFrom event 
81.                 // handler). In a real app, whether and when to clear an override will depend on
82.                 // the desired behaviour and the design of the app.
83. 
84.                 var context = ResourceContext.GetForCurrentView();
85. 
86.                 var selectedLanguage = Scenario9ComboBox.SelectedValue;
87.                 if (selectedLanguage != null)
88.                 {
89.                     var lang = new List<string>();
90.                     lang.Add(selectedLanguage.ToString());
91.                     context.Languages = lang;
92.                     var resourceStringMap = ResourceManager.Current.MainResourceMap.GetSubtree("Resources");
93.                     this.Scenario9TextBlock.Text = resourceStringMap.GetValue("string1", context).ValueAsString;
94.                 }
95.             }
96.         }
97. 
98.     }
99. }

1. ﻿<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8"?>
2. <root>
3.   <!-- 
4.     Microsoft ResX Schema 
5.     
6.     Version 2.0
7.     
8.     The primary goals of this format is to allow a simple XML format 
9.     that is mostly human readable. The generation and parsing of the 
10.     various data types are done through the TypeConverter classes 
11.     associated with the data types.
12.     
13.     Example:
14.     
15.     ... ado.net/XML headers & schema ...
16.     <resheader name="resmimetype">text/microsoft-resx</resheader>
17.     <resheader name="version">2.0</resheader>
18.     <resheader name="reader">System.Resources.ResXResourceReader, System.Windows.Forms, ...</resheader>
19.     <resheader name="writer">System.Resources.ResXResourceWriter, System.Windows.Forms, ...</resheader>
20.     <data name="Name1"><value>this is my long string</value><comment>this is a comment</comment></data>
21.     <data name="Color1" type="System.Drawing.Color, System.Drawing">Blue</data>
22.     <data name="Bitmap1" mimetype="application/x-microsoft.net.object.binary.base64">
23.         <value>[base64 mime encoded serialized .NET Framework object]</value>
24.     </data>
25.     <data name="Icon1" type="System.Drawing.Icon, System.Drawing" mimetype="application/x-microsoft.net.object.bytearray.base64">
26.         <value>[base64 mime encoded string representing a byte array form of the .NET Framework object]</value>
27.         <comment>This is a comment</comment>
28.     </data>
29.                 
30.     There are any number of "resheader" rows that contain simple 
31.     name/value pairs.
32.     
33.     Each data row contains a name, and value. The row also contains a 
34.     type or mimetype. Type corresponds to a .NET class that support 
35.     text/value conversion through the TypeConverter architecture. 
36.     Classes that don't support this are serialized and stored with the 
37.     mimetype set.
38.     
39.     The mimetype is used for serialized objects, and tells the 
40.     ResXResourceReader how to depersist the object. This is currently not 
41.     extensible. For a given mimetype the value must be set accordingly:
42.     
43.     Note - application/x-microsoft.net.object.binary.base64 is the format 
44.     that the ResXResourceWriter will generate, however the reader can 
45.     read any of the formats listed below.
46.     
47.     mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.binary.base64
48.     value   : The object must be serialized with 
49.             : System.Runtime.Serialization.Formatters.Binary.BinaryFormatter
50.             : and then encoded with base64 encoding.
51.     
52.     mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.soap.base64
53.     value   : The object must be serialized with 
54.             : System.Runtime.Serialization.Formatters.Soap.SoapFormatter
55.             : and then encoded with base64 encoding.
56. 
57.     mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.bytearray.base64
58.     value   : The object must be serialized into a byte array 
59.             : using a System.ComponentModel.TypeConverter
60.             : and then encoded with base64 encoding.
61.     -->
62.   <xsd:schema id="root" xmlns="" xmlns:xsd="http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema" xmlns:msdata="urn:schemas-microsoft-com:xml-msdata">

3.   <!-- 
4.     Microsoft ResX Schema 
5.     
6.     Version 2.0
7.     
8.     The primary goals of this format is to allow a simple XML format 
9.     that is mostly human readable. The generation and parsing of the 
10.     various data types are done through the TypeConverter classes 
11.     associated with the data types.
12.     
13.     Example:
14.     
15.     ... ado.net/XML headers & schema ...
16.     <resheader name="resmimetype">text/microsoft-resx</resheader>
17.     <resheader name="version">2.0</resheader>
18.     <resheader name="reader">System.Resources.ResXResourceReader, System.Windows.Forms, ...</resheader>
19.     <resheader name="writer">System.Resources.ResXResourceWriter, System.Windows.Forms, ...</resheader>
20.     <data name="Name1"><value>this is my long string</value><comment>this is a comment</comment></data>
21.     <data name="Color1" type="System.Drawing.Color, System.Drawing">Blue</data>
22.     <data name="Bitmap1" mimetype="application/x-microsoft.net.object.binary.base64">
23.         <value>[base64 mime encoded serialized .NET Framework object]</value>
24.     </data>
25.     <data name="Icon1" type="System.Drawing.Icon, System.Drawing" mimetype="application/x-microsoft.net.object.bytearray.base64">
26.         <value>[base64 mime encoded string representing a byte array form of the .NET Framework object]</value>
27.         <comment>This is a comment</comment>
28.     </data>
29.                 
30.     There are any number of "resheader" rows that contain simple 
31.     name/value pairs.
32.     
33.     Each data row contains a name, and value. The row also contains a 
34.     type or mimetype. Type corresponds to a .NET class that support 
35.     text/value conversion through the TypeConverter architecture. 
36.     Classes that don't support this are serialized and stored with the 
37.     mimetype set.
38.     
39.     The mimetype is used for serialized objects, and tells the 
40.     ResXResourceReader how to depersist the object. This is currently not 
41.     extensible. For a given mimetype the value must be set accordingly:
42.     
43.     Note - application/x-microsoft.net.object.binary.base64 is the format 
44.     that the ResXResourceWriter will generate, however the reader can 
45.     read any of the formats listed below.
46.     
47.     mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.binary.base64
48.     value   : The object must be serialized with 
49.             : System.Runtime.Serialization.Formatters.Binary.BinaryFormatter
50.             : and then encoded with base64 encoding.
51.     
52.     mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.soap.base64
53.     value   : The object must be serialized with 
54.             : System.Runtime.Serialization.Formatters.Soap.SoapFormatter
55.             : and then encoded with base64 encoding.
56. 
57.     mimetype: application/x-microsoft.net.object.bytearray.base64
58.     value   : The object must be serialized into a byte array 
59.             : using a System.ComponentModel.TypeConverter
60.             : and then encoded with base64 encoding.
61.     -->

22.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
23.     /// </summary>
24.     public sealed partial class Scenario10 : Page
25.     {
26.         // A pointer back to the main page.  This is needed if you want to call methods in MainPage such
27.         // as NotifyUser()
28.         MainPage rootPage = MainPage.Current;
29. 
30.         public Scenario10()
31.         {
32.             this.InitializeComponent();
33.         }
34. 
35.         /// <summary>
36.         /// Invoked when this page is about to be displayed in a Frame.
37.         /// </summary>
38.         /// <param name="e">Event data that describes how this page was reached.  The Parameter
39.         /// property is typically used to configure the page.</param>
40.         protected override void OnNavigatedTo(NavigationEventArgs e)
41.         {
42.         }
43. 
44.         /// <summary>
45.         /// This is the click handler for the 'Scenario10Button_Show' button.  You would replace this with your own handler
46.         /// if you have a button or buttons on this page.
47.         /// </summary>
48.         /// <param name="sender"></param>
49.         /// <param name="e"></param>
50.         private void Scenario10Button_Show_Click(object sender, RoutedEventArgs e)
51.         {
52.             Button b = sender as Button;
53.             if (b != null)
54.             {
55.                 // use a cloned context for this scenario so that qualifier values set
56.                 // in this scenario don't impact behaviour in other scenarios that
57.                 // use a default context for the view (crossover effects between
58.                 // the scenarios will not be expected)
59.                 // TODO[migrate-002]: see PATTERNS.md#getforcurrentview
60.                 var context = ResourceContext.GetForCurrentView().Clone();
61. 
62.                 var selectedLanguage = Scenario10ComboBox_Language.SelectedValue;
63.                 var selectedScale = Scenario10ComboBox_Scale.SelectedValue;
64.                 var selectedContrast = Scenario10ComboBox_Contrast.SelectedValue;
65.                 var selectedHomeRegion = Scenario10ComboBox_HomeRegion.SelectedValue;
66. 
67.                 if (selectedLanguage != null)
68.                 {
69.                     context.QualifierValues["language"] = selectedLanguage.ToString();
70.                 }
71.                 if (selectedScale != null)
72.                 {
73.                     context.QualifierValues["scale"] = selectedScale.ToString();
74.                 }
75.                 if (selectedContrast != null)
76.                 {
77.                     context.QualifierValues["contrast"] = selectedContrast.ToString();
78.                 }
79.                 if (selectedHomeRegion != null)
80.                 {
81.                     context.QualifierValues["homeregion"] = selectedHomeRegion.ToString();
82.                 }
83. 
84.                 Scenario10_SearchMultipleResourceIds(context, new string[] { "LanguageOnly", "ScaleOnly", "ContrastOnly", "HomeRegionOnly", "MultiDimensional" });
85.             }
86.         }
87. 
88.         void Scenario10_SearchMultipleResourceIds(ResourceContext context, string[] resourceIds)
89.         {
90.             this.Scenario10TextBlock.Text = "";
91.             var dimensionMap = ResourceManager.Current.MainResourceMap.GetSubtree("dimensions");
92. 
93.             foreach (var id in resourceIds)
94.             {
95.                 NamedResource namedResource;
96.                 if (dimensionMap.TryGetValue(id, out namedResource))
97.                 {
98.                     var resourceCandidates = namedResource.ResolveAll(context);
99.                     Scenario10_ShowCandidates(id, resourceCandidates);
100.                 }
101.             }
102.         }
103. 
104.         void Scenario10_ShowCandidates(string resourceId, IReadOnlyList<ResourceCandidate> resourceCandidates)
105.         {
106.             // print 'resourceId', 'found value', 'qualifer info', 'matching condition'
107.             string outText = "resourceId: dimensions\\" + resourceId + "\r\n";
108.             int i = 0;
109.             foreach (var candidate in resourceCandidates)
110.             {
111.                 var value = candidate.ValueAsString;
112.                 outText += "    Candidate " + i.ToString() + ":" + value + "\r\n";
113.                 int j = 0;
114.                 foreach (var qualifier in candidate.Qualifiers)
115.                 {
116.                     var qualifierName = qualifier.QualifierName;
117.                     var qualifierValue = qualifier.QualifierValue;
118.                     outText += "        Qualifer: " + qualifierName + ": " + qualifierValue + "\r\n";
119.                     outText += "        Matching: IsMatch (" + qualifier.IsMatch.ToString() + ")  " + "IsDefault (" + qualifier.IsDefault.ToString() + ")" + "\r\n";
120.                     j++;
121.                 }
122.                 i++;
123.             }
124. 
125.             this.Scenario10TextBlock.Text += outText + "\r\n";
126. 
127.         }
128. 
129.     }
130. }

22.     /// <summary>
23.     /// An empty page that can be used on its own or navigated to within a Frame.
24.     /// </summary>
25.     public sealed partial class Scenario8 : Page
26.     {
27.         int lastSelectedIndex;
28. 
29.         public Scenario8()
30.         {
31.             this.InitializeComponent();
32.             PopulateComboBox();
33.             UpdateCurrentAppLanguageMessage();
34.         }
35. 
36.         private void ShowText()
37.         {
38.             // TODO[migrate-010]: see PATTERNS.md#getforcurrentview
39.             ResourceContext defaultContextForCurrentView = ResourceContext.GetForCurrentView();
40.             ResourceMap stringResourcesResourceMap = ResourceManager.Current.MainResourceMap.GetSubtree("Resources");
41.             Scenario8MessageTextBlock.Text = stringResourcesResourceMap.GetValue("string1", defaultContextForCurrentView).ValueAsString;
42.         }
43. 
44.         private void LanguageOverrideCombo_SelectionChanged(object sender, SelectionChangedEventArgs e)
45.         {
46.             var combo = sender as ComboBox;
47.             var item = combo.SelectedValue as ComboBoxItem;
48.             var languageTag = item.Tag as string;
49. 
50.             // Ignore the divider (tag = "-")
51.             if (languageTag == "-")
52.             {
53.                 combo.SelectedIndex = lastSelectedIndex;
54.             }
55.             else
56.             {
57.                 lastSelectedIndex = combo.SelectedIndex;
58. 
59.                 // Set the persistent language override
60.                 ApplicationLanguages.PrimaryLanguageOverride = languageTag;
61. 
62.                 // update current app languages message
63.                 UpdateCurrentAppLanguageMessage();
64.             }
65.         }
66. 
67.         void AddItemForLanguageTag(string languageTag)
68.         {
69.             var language = new Language(languageTag);
70.             var item = new ComboBoxItem { Content = language.DisplayName, Tag = languageTag };
71.             LanguageOverrideCombo.Items.Add(item);
72. 
73.             // Select this item if it is the primary language override.
74.             if (languageTag == ApplicationLanguages.PrimaryLanguageOverride)
75.             {
76.                 LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectedItem = item;
77.             }
78.         }
79. 
80.         void PopulateComboBox()
81.         {
82.             // First show the default setting
83.             LanguageOverrideCombo.Items.Add(new ComboBoxItem { Content = "Use language preferences (recommended)", Tag = "" });
84.             LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectedIndex = 0;
85. 
86.             // If there are app languages that the user speaks, show them next
87. 
88.             // Note: the first (non-override) language, if set as the primary language override
89.             // would give the same result as not having any primary language override. There's
90.             // still a difference, though: If the user changes their language preferences, the 
91.             // default setting (no override) would mean that the actual primary app language
92.             // could change. But if it's set as an override, then it will remain the primary
93.             // app language after the user changes their language preferences.
94. 
95.             foreach (var languageTag in ApplicationLanguages.Languages)
96.             {
97.                 AddItemForLanguageTag(languageTag);
98.             }
99. 
100.             // Now add a divider followed by all the app manifest languages (in case the user
101.             // wants to pick a language not currently in their profile)
102. 
103.             // NOTE: If an app is deployed using a bundle with resource packages, the following
104.             // addition to the list may not be useful: The set of languages returned by 
105.             // ApplicationLanguages.ManifestLanguages will consist of only those manifest 
106.             // languages in the main app package or in the resource packages that are installed 
107.             // and registered for the current user. Language resource packages get deployed for 
108.             // the user if the language is in the user's profile. Therefore, the only difference 
109.             // from the set returned by ApplicationLanguages.Languages above would depend on 
110.             // which languages are included in the main app package.
111. 
112.             LanguageOverrideCombo.Items.Add(new ComboBoxItem { Content = "——————", Tag = "-" });
113. 
114.             // In a production app, this list should be sorted, but that's beyond the
115.             // focus of this sample.
116.             foreach (var languageTag in ApplicationLanguages.ManifestLanguages)
117.             {
118.                 AddItemForLanguageTag(languageTag);
119.             }
120. 
121.             LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectionChanged += LanguageOverrideCombo_SelectionChanged;
122. 
123.             lastSelectedIndex = LanguageOverrideCombo.SelectedIndex;
124.         }
125. 
126.         private void UpdateCurrentAppLanguageMessage()
127.         {
128.             this.Scenario8AppLanguagesTextBlock.Text = "Current app language(s): " + GetAppLanguagesAsFormattedString();
129.         }
130. 
131.         private string GetAppLanguagesAsFormattedString()
132.         {
133.             return String.Join(", ", ApplicationLanguages.Languages);
134.         }
135.     }
136. }
